❯ first describe me suppose one nightly retrain and revision is done, I switch  
  on the product next day, what it does and how long it runs and does the job,  
  what exactly does it do no technical terms , normal lang                      

● Fair question. Here's the day, in plain language.

  The night before (you're asleep)

  Around midnight it wakes up on its own and does four things:

  1. Downloads the day's price history for the ~250 stocks it watches — every
  minute of trading, ~375 data points per stock.
  2. Grades yesterday's suggestions. It said "buy X, get out at ₹Y, target ₹Z."
  It now looks at what actually happened minute by minute and marks each one:
  hit target, hit stop, or went nowhere. It cannot cheat here — it wrote down
  its reasoning before the day started and it's scored against that frozen copy.
  3. Relearns, now including yesterday.
  4. Checks whether the new version is actually better than the one it's been 
  using. If it isn't, it throws the new one away and keeps the old one. This is
  the part that matters most and it's the part most systems skip.

  Takes roughly 20–40 minutes. You never see it.

  Morning — you switch it on

  Before 9:15 — nothing useful yet. The market hasn't opened. Anything it told
  you now would be guessing at the gap.

  9:15–9:30 — deliberately silent. The first fifteen minutes are the noisiest of
  the day; a lot of what looks like a move is just overnight orders clearing.
  It's watching, not talking.

  ~9:30 — the list appears. This is the moment you actually use the product. You
  get something like:

  ▎ TATAMOTORS — buy near ₹982, get out at ₹967, target ₹1,010, buy 38 shares
  ▎ Why: broke above its opening range on 2.4× normal volume, sitting above its 
  ▎ average price for the day, Nifty is green.

  Two to four names. Not twenty. On a quiet day, possibly zero — and a day where
  it says "nothing worth trading" is it working correctly, not failing. Most of
  what makes money at this size is skipping bad days.

  The share count is already worked out for your ₹15,000 at 5×, including how
  many of those names you can hold at once before you run out of margin.

  You place the orders. It does not touch your Angel One account. It suggests;
  you decide and execute.

  During the day

  It watches your positions every few seconds and pings you for exactly three
  reasons:

  - your stop got hit — get out now
  - your target got hit — take it
  - you've been in this for an hour and it's gone nowhere — the reason you 
  entered has expired, get out

  That last one is the unglamorous one that protects small accounts. Money tied
  up in a stock doing nothing is money not available for the setup at 1pm.

  3:15pm — anything still open gets flagged hard. Intraday positions get
  force-closed by the broker around 3:20 at whatever price is going, so it makes
  sure you close on your own terms first.

  Your actual attention: about five minutes at 9:30, then only when it pings 
  you.

  After close

  A short honest scorecard: what it suggested, what you actually did, what each
  made or lost after brokerage — and the running record. Not "12 signals
  generated." More like: "Suggested 3. You took 2. Net +₹740 after costs. 22-day
  running average: +₹310/day."

  ---
  Two honest things about this

  The first stretch is paper. It suggests and grades itself, but you don't have
  to act on any of it. That's not caution theatre — it's the only way to find
  out what the thing actually earns. A strategy needs a few weeks of graded days
  before the average means anything.

  And that average may not be ₹1,500. Right now nobody knows the number,
  including me — that's what the research phase measures. The system is built to
  tell you the truth about what it earns, not to reach for the figure you
  named. If it turns out to be ₹400/day on ₹15,000, it'll say ₹400/day, and
  that's still a genuinely good return on that capital — it's just not the
  number we started with.

  Want me to go back into the plan, or adjust anything about how the day works
  before I do?

❯ its a proper quant brain pipeline that is doing the job right?                

● Yes — but let me be precise about which half is proper, because it matters.

  What's genuinely institutional here

  The evaluation machinery in this repo is better disciplined than most retail
  algo projects and a fair number of small funds:

  It tests on time, not on shuffled data. Standard machine learning splits data
  randomly — which, on markets, means training on Thursday to predict Wednesday.
  This uses expanding walk-forward with a purge and embargo: train on the past,
  leave a gap, test on the future, never the reverse. You even found and fixed
  a bug where the gap was 13× too conservative.

  It knows what was tradeable then, not now. The point-in-time universe is the
  thing almost nobody does. If you test a strategy on today's stock list, you've
  secretly told it which companies didn't go bankrupt. Your bhavcopy spine
  grows 1,545 → 4,661 names across 16 years because it's a real historical
  record, including the failures.

  It adjusts prices from primary source and proved it. 34,829 corporate actions
  parsed from NSE's own feed, validated at 96.2% against an independent source —
  with the four misses individually named rather than averaged away.

  It costs trades to the paisa. Not "assume 20bps." An actual charge model
  checked against a real broker sheet.

  And the one that actually separates research from self-deception: the gate is 
  pre-registered. Thresholds written down and committed before the results
  exist. You caught yourself doing this wrong once — thresholds picked after
  seeing the numbers — and instead of quietly fixing it, you documented it as a
  failure and rebuilt the criteria from statistical principle. That's the single
  most credible thing in the whole project.

  Plus: leakage tests, determinism tests, an adversarial pass that tries to
  falsify a result before it's accepted, frozen prediction snapshots so grading
  can't be revised with hindsight, and kill criteria written in advance.

  That is a proper quant research pipeline. Not a wrapper around RSI crossovers.

  What isn't proven yet

  The scaffolding is real. The edge is the open question, and I don't want you
  to conflate the two:

  - The brain itself is one gradient-boosted tree ranking stocks
  cross-sectionally. That's a legitimate, standard workhorse — not exotic, and
  not a weakness. But it's one model, one strategy. Real quant desks run many
  weakly-correlated signals so no single one failing hurts much.
  - The only edge ever measured is +0.394% per month, on 98 survivor-biased
  symbols. Thin. The honest re-run on the full universe — the one that would
  confirm or kill it — is the work we just parked.
  - There is no intraday edge measured at all. Zero. That's the entire Phase E,
  and it's genuinely uncertain: at 8.3bps a round trip and daily turnover, cost
  drag is roughly an order of magnitude harsher relative to holding period than
  it was monthly.
  - No real fills yet, so no measured slippage.

  The honest summary

  You have a rigorous laboratory and an unproven hypothesis. That's the right
  way round — the expensive mistake is the opposite, a great-looking strategy
  with no way to tell if it's real.

  The lab is what will tell you, in a few weeks of graded days, whether intraday
  signals on Indian equities clear costs at your size. It's equally capable of
  telling you no, which is exactly why it's worth trusting when it says yes.

  Want me to re-submit the plan for approval, or dig into any part of it first?

● Good question, and the honest answer starts with a reality check — because two
  of the four components you named don't exist yet.

  What's actually there right now

  Component: LightGBM
  Status: ✅ Real — models/ranker.py
  What it does today: The actual brain. Predicts cross-sectional relative
  forward
  return; top-N by score becomes the picks
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Component: Claude
  Status: ✅ Real — agent/claude_cli.py
  What it does today: Writes explanations. Locked down (see below)
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Component: Ollama
  Status: ⚠️ Half — referenced in rag/embed.py, not installed, server not 
  running
  What it does today: Intended for local embeddings (nomic-embed-text). Only
  qwen2.5:3b was ever pulled — a chat model, no embedding capability
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Component: CatBoost
  Status: ❌ Doesn't exist
  What it does today: Not installed, not referenced anywhere
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Component: Your GPU
  Status: RTX 3050, 4GB
  What it does today: Confirmed. This is the binding constraint on anything
  local-LLM

  So when I describe the nightly run below, I'll mark what's built versus what
  Phase E adds.

  ---
  The single most important rule in the design

  Before the pipeline: no language model ever produces a number that decides a 
  trade.

  Your codebase already enforces this and it's the best thing in it.
  agent/claude_cli.py serializes facts as the only permitted source of figures,
  and a numeric tripwire flags any number in Claude's response that can't be
  traced to those facts.

  - LightGBM decides. What to buy, what rank, what score.
  - Python decides. Position size, stop, target, cost.
  - Claude explains. In English, after the fact, and cannot change anything.
  - Ollama scores news sentiment — which becomes one input column the model may
  or may not find useful, tested like any other feature.

  The moment an LLM is allowed to say "I think RELIANCE looks good," you no
  longer have a quant system, you have a chatbot with a database. That line is
  already drawn and Phase E keeps it.

  ---
  The nightly run, step by step

  Kicks off around midnight. Each step writes a heartbeat row, so if it dies at
  3am you know exactly where.

  1. Fetch (~5–10 min) — Yesterday's 1-minute bars for the ~250 watched stocks,
  plus the daily bhavcopy for context. Resumable: interrupt it and it picks up
  where it stopped, not from scratch. (Phase E1)

  2. Grade yesterday (~seconds) — Every suggestion it made was frozen at 9:30
  with a hash of the exact inputs. Now it replays the actual minute-by-minute
  path and marks each: target first, stop first, or timed out. It cannot revise 
  what it predicted — that's what the hash is for. (Built — harness/loops.py)

  3. Build features (~10–20 min) — Roll 1-min into 5-min, compute opening-range
  position, VWAP distance, volume-spike-vs-same-time-of-day, gap, prior-day
  range, index direction, time-of-day. (Phase E2)

  4. Retrain (~3–5 min) — Refit on all history including yesterday. Measured on
  your machine: 5–17 seconds per fit at 3.3M rows, so this is cheap.

  5. Champion vs challenger (~seconds) — This is the step that makes it a quant 
  system rather than a script. The fresh model doesn't automatically take over.
  It has to beat the one currently in use, on the pre-registered criteria in
  gate.py, or it gets archived and last night's model keeps running. Most nights
  nothing changes. That's correct. (Built)

  6. Write the scorecard (~1 min) — Python computes every figure; Claude turns
  them into a paragraph you'll actually read.

  Total: 20–40 minutes, unattended.

  One distinction that matters: nightly retrain ≠ nightly re-validation. The
  full purged walk-forward across all folds is a weekly or monthly job (1–2
  hours), not nightly. Re-running the full validation every night and watching
  for a good result is just slow overfitting.

  ---
  During market hours — your actual question

  You asked whether it keeps researching live. Here's the split, and it's
  deliberate:

  It does NOT learn during the day. The model is frozen at 9:15 and does not 
  change until midnight.

  That's not a limitation, it's the whole point. A model that retrains on this
  morning's data to trade this afternoon has seen the answer. It will look
  brilliant in testing and lose money live. Frozen-at-open is non-negotiable.

  It DOES score live, continuously. Every 5 minutes it re-runs the frozen model
  over fresh bars. So:

  - 9:30 — the opening list. 2–4 names, or none.
  - 10:15, 11:40, 1:20… — new setups as they form. A stock that was nothing at
  9:30 can break its range at 11:00 and appear then. This is your "capturing
  live signals" — and it's legitimate, because applying a fixed model to new
  data is exactly what it'll do every day forever.
  - Continuously — the fast stop/target monitor. Your deleted v1 had this at a
  7-second poll (intraday_stops.py, recoverable from git). A stop breached at
  10:31 can't wait for the next 5-minute cycle.

  The rule in one line: re-scoring during the day is fine, re-training during 
  the day is forbidden.

  ---
  Where CatBoost and Ollama actually fit

  CatBoost — worth adding, but as a measured addition, not an assumed one.

  It's genuinely different from LightGBM (ordered boosting, symmetric trees), so
  a two-model average usually picks up a little robustness. But it doubles
  training time and adds a second artifact to version, and it's a marginal gain
  compared to the open question of whether any intraday edge exists.

  So: build the pipeline model-agnostic, prove or kill the edge with LightGBM
  alone, then add CatBoost as an ablation under the identical pre-registered 
  gate — it has to earn its place with a measured improvement, not get in
  because ensembles are conventionally better. Same rule that governs the
  delivery-% feature.

  Ollama — real job, tightly scoped, and 4GB is the constraint.

  Your RTX 3050 has 4GB. A 7B model won't fit comfortably; you'd be on
  qwen2.5:3b or smaller, and it'd contend with nothing else running. Given that,
  its honest scope is:

  1. Embeddings for the news/document store — needs ollama pull 
  nomic-embed-text, 274MB, works on 4GB fine.
  2. Sector/news sentiment — turn overnight headlines into a per-sector score
  that becomes a feature column.

  Not price prediction. Not stock selection. A 3B local model has no edge on
  price that a gradient-boosted tree on real order-flow features doesn't have
  more reliably.

  Notably, rag/embed.py already fails soft — returns None when Ollama is down
  rather than crashing. So the pipeline runs fine with Ollama switched off
  entirely, which is the right default until the sentiment feature proves
  itself.

  ---
  How you audit it

  Four things, in rough order of how often you'd look:

  1. The daily scorecard — suggested vs taken vs realized, net of costs, plus
  the running average. The only number that matters.
  2. The frozen snapshots — pick any past suggestion, re-run the grader, confirm
  the hash matches. Proves nothing was recomputed with hindsight.
  3. The champion/challenger log — how often the new model actually wins. If
  it's replacing itself every single night, something's unstable. If it never
  wins, learning has stalled.
  4. Feature importance drift — if the model suddenly leans hard on a feature it
  ignored for months, that's usually a data bug, not an insight. This is where
  tree models earn their keep over neural nets: you can actually look.

  Plus the standing guardrails: leakage tests, determinism tests, the
  adversarial pass, and gate.py as a protected file that changes only through a
  reviewed PR.

  ---
  Build order

  E0 tradebook forensics + E1 Upstox backfill (parallel — backfill runs 
  unattended) → E2 intraday features and the path-dependent stop/target label →
  E3 pre-registration, then the walk-forward, then the verdict → E4 the daily
  loop, paper-first, only if E3 passes.

  The gate between E3 and E4 is real. If intraday signals don't clear 8.3bps, E4
  doesn't get built, and that's a result worth having for the cost of E1–E3.

  Want me to fold this component detail into the plan file and re-submit it?

● Audit complete, and it turned up material gaps. Confirmed by reading the code,
  not inferring:

  - tax.py:classify_positions filters to segment == "equity_delivery" and
  explicitly excludes intraday — so on an intraday-only book it computes ₹0 tax.
  Intraday profit is speculative business income at your slab rate, not 20%
  STCG.
  - Zero hits for circuit anywhere in src/. No daily-loss-limit control exists
  (counterfactuals.py is retrospective analysis, not a live guard).
  - risk_layer.py has good primitives (position/sector/correlation caps) but
  they're delivery-shaped and called from no intraday path.
  - Good news: compute_charges does handle equity_intraday correctly — STT
  2.5bps sell-leg-only, stamp duty buy-only.

     │   │                                                                     │
     │ ├───────────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────── │
     │ ───┤                                                                    │
     │ │           │                           │ Explains only. Facts are the  │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │ Claude    │ ✅ agent/claude_cli.py    │ sole permitted source of      │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │           │                           │ figures; a numeric tripwire   │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │           │                           │ flags any untraceable number  │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ ├───────────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────── │
     │ ───┤                                                                    │
     │ │           │ ⚠️ referenced in          │ News/sector sentiment +       │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │ Ollama    │ rag/embed.py, not         │ embeddings. embed_text        │
     │ already  │                                                              │
     │ │           │ installed, server down    │ fails soft (returns None), so │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │           │                           │ the pipeline runs with it off │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ ├───────────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────── │
     │ ───┤                                                                    │
     │ │           │                           │ Optional later — as a         │
     │ measured  │                                                             │
     │ │ CatBoost  │ ❌ absent                 │ ablation under the same gate, │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │           │                           │ never an assumed improvement  │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ ├───────────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────────────── │
     │ ───┤                                                                    │
     │ │           │                           │ Binding constraint. 7B won't  │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │ GPU       │ RTX 3050 4GB (confirmed)  │ fit; qwen2.5:3b /             │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ │           │                           │ nomic-embed-text (274MB) will │
     │   │                                                                     │
     │ └───────────┴───────────────────────────┴────────────────────────────── │
     │ ───┘                                                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ The load-bearing rule: no language model ever produces a number that    │
     │ decides a trade. LightGBM decides what. Python decides size, stop,      │
     │ target, cost. Claude writes prose after the fact. Ollama contributes    │
     │ one sentiment column tested like any other feature. This is already     │
     │ enforced in claude_cli.py and Phase E keeps it.                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ Audit findings — the gaps between "a backtest" and "a profit-making     │
     │ pipeline"                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Verified by reading code this session. These are the difference between │
     │ a strategy that looks good and one that survives contact with money.    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ #: A1                                                                   │
     │ Gap: No account-level daily loss limit / kill switch                    │
     │ Evidence: grep daily_loss|max_loss|loss_limit → only                    │
     │ statements/counterfactuals.py, which is retrospective                   │
     │ Why it costs money: The single most important control for a 5×          │
     │ leveraged ₹15k                                                          │
     │ account. Without it, one bad day compounds into a blown account         │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: A2                                                                   │
     │ Gap: Intraday tax unmodeled — reports ₹0                                │
     │ Evidence: tax.py:classify_positions filters segment ==                  │
     │ "equity_delivery";                                                      │
     │ comment concedes intraday is "a different regime this module doesn't    │
     │ attempt                                                                 │
     │ to model"                                                               │
     │ Why it costs money: Intraday = speculative business income at your slab │
     │ rate                                                                    │
     │ (up to 30% + 4% cess), not 20% STCG. Changes ₹1,500/day gross into      │
     │ materially                                                              │
     │  less take-home                                                         │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: A3                                                                   │
     │ Gap: Slippage is a flat constant; no participation check                │
     │ Evidence: cost_model.indian_delivery_cost_bps(slippage_bps=5.0) —       │
     │ "modeled, not                                                           │
     │ replayed"                                                               │
     │ Why it costs money: No check on what share of a bar's volume your order │
     │ is. The                                                                 │
     │  #1 way intraday backtests overstate returns                            │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: A4                                                                   │
     │ Gap: No MIS-eligibility / leverage list                                 │
     │ Evidence: absent                                                        │
     │ Why it costs money: Not every stock gets 5×; the list is                │
     │ broker-specific and                                                     │
     │ changes. Sizing assuming uniform 5× is simply wrong on many names       │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: A5                                                                   │
     │ Gap: No circuit-band handling                                           │
     │ Evidence: grep circuit in src/ → zero hits                              │
     │ Why it costs money: A stock locked at upper circuit can't be bought. A  │
     │ 5% band                                                                 │
     │ stock behaves nothing like a 20% one. Both are hard filters             │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: A6                                                                   │
     │ Gap: Risk layer is delivery-shaped and unwired                          │
     │ Evidence: optimizer/risk_layer.py has position (15%) / sector (35%) /   │
     │ correlation-cluster (50%) caps — called from no intraday path           │
     │ Why it costs money: At 5× across 2–4 names, "max 15% weight" is         │
     │ meaningless.                                                            │
     │ Limits must be in equity-at-risk, not weight                            │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: A7                                                                   │
     │ Gap: No regime stand-down                                               │
     │ Evidence: absent                                                        │
     │ Why it costs money: Nothing says "conditions are hostile today, don't   │
     │ trade"                                                                  │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: A8                                                                   │
     │ Gap: No live data-staleness detection                                   │
     │ Evidence: absent                                                        │
     │ Why it costs money: If the feed dies at 11:00, nothing notices and      │
     │ stale prices                                                            │
     │ drive decisions                                                         │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: A9                                                                   │
     │ Gap: No live model-decay alarm                                          │
     │ Evidence: champion/challenger is nightly only                           │
     │ Why it costs money: A model degrading over three weeks looks fine each  │
     │ individual night                                                        │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ #: A10                                                                  │
     │ Gap: Fill price unspecified                                             │
     │ Evidence: —                                                             │
     │ Why it costs money: A backtest filling at this bar's close trades at a  │
     │ price                                                                   │
     │ that already happened. Must be next-bar open + half-spread              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Good news from the audit: compute_charges already handles               │
     │ equity_intraday correctly (STT 2.5bps sell-leg only, stamp duty         │
     │ buy-only) — verified. That's the load-bearing cost term and it's right. │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ Build order                                                             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ E0 + E1 run in parallel (E1's backfill is unattended). E3 must land     │
     │ before E4's verdict — a backtest without execution realism measures a   │
     │ fantasy. E5 must land before E6 touches real money.                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ E0 — Ground truth from your own trades                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ statements table is empty; the forensics have never seen real data.     │
     │ - Export your Angel One (+ Groww) tradebook. Run stocksense             │
     │ statement-ingest <file> then stocksense kundli.                         │
     │ - Reuse src/stocksense/statements/ as-is — fuzzy-column parsers, FIFO   │
     │ reconstruction, 13-metric dosha catalogue, counterfactual engine.       │
     │ Angel's format likely needs a new parser variant; follow the existing   │
     │ golden-file test pattern.                                               │
     │ - Output: real per-day P&L distribution, win rate, avg win vs avg loss, │
     │ expectancy, actual cost drag, max drawdown — and behavioral             │
     │ diagnostics (revenge trading, sizing chaos). Every sizing rule          │
     │ downstream calibrates to these.                                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ E1 — Intraday data spine (Upstox 1-minute)                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ New: src/stocksense/data/upstox_intraday.py                             │
     │ - Reuse nse_archive.py patterns: _cached_or_fetch (nse_archive.py:53)   │
     │ for hash-keyed disk cache + politeness, and critically the generator +  │
     │ incremental-write shape of fetch_range — the eager version is what lost │
     │ an entire backfill to one interruption (731c262). Resumability is a     │
     │ property, not a nicety.                                                 │
     │ - Instrument mapping: bhavcopy_eq has no ISIN column (verified schema:  │
     │ symbol, series, date, open, high, low, close, prev_close, volume,       │
     │ turnover_inr, era), and Upstox keys as NSE_EQ|<ISIN>. Fetch and cache   │
     │ Upstox's instrument master; build symbol → instrument_key; record       │
     │ unmapped symbols loudly, never silently skip.                           │
     │ - New table intraday_bars(symbol, ts, interval, open, high, low, close, │
     │ volume) + write_/read_intraday_bars following write_bhavcopy_eq         │
     │ (store.py:698).                                                         │
     │ - Universe: top ~250 by trailing turnover, resolved point-in-time via   │
     │ existing universe_pit.universe_as_of /                                  │
     │ filter_to_point_in_time_universe. (1,406 EQ names clear ₹2cr turnover   │
     │ on a recent day — 250 is a deliberate, liquid subset.)                  │
     │ - CLI: stocksense backfill-intraday --start 2022-01-01 --end <today>,   │
     │ mirroring backfill_nse_archive_cmd (cli/main.py:389).                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ E2 — Intraday features and path-dependent labels                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ New: src/stocksense/features/intraday.py. Recover SKILL_intraday.md     │
     │ from 06aed58 as the spec.                                               │
     │ - Opening-range position (first 15-min high/low, break on >1.5×         │
     │ volume); VWAP distance + RSI; volume spike vs same-time-of-day baseline │
     │ (intraday volume is U-shaped — a flat baseline fires at every open and  │
     │ close); explicit time-of-day; index direction; and prior-day context    │
     │ from bhavcopy (gap, prior range/ATR, delivery-%).                       │
     │ - Labels — where intraday genuinely differs. Daily labels are           │
     │ close-to-close and path-independent. Intraday P&L is path-dependent:    │
     │ with a 1.5% stop, whether price touched −1.5% before +2% decides        │
     │ everything. Build two:                                                  │
     │   a. fwd_ret_<n>bars — analogue of the existing label, for ranking/IC.  │
     │   b. First-touch stop/target label — simulated bar-by-bar against the   │
     │ actual 1-minute path (this is why 1-min is stored even though research  │
     │ is 5-min). Stop-first, target-first, or time-exit.                      │
     │ - Both session-bounded — never cross 15:30. An MIS position does not    │
     │ exist overnight.                                                        │
     │                                                                         │
     │ E3 — Execution realism (new — closes A3, A4, A5, A10)                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Without this the verdict is fiction. New                                │
     │ src/stocksense/execution/fill_model.py:                                 │
     │ - Explicit fill price: next-bar open + half-spread. Never this-bar      │
     │ close. (A10)                                                            │
     │ - Participation cap: reject any simulated fill exceeding a set share of │
     │ that bar's actual volume; at ₹75k this rarely binds on liquid names,    │
     │ but it must be checked, not assumed. (A3)                               │
     │ - Circuit-band filter: exclude bars where the stock is locked or in a   │
     │ narrow band, derived from bhavcopy price bands. (A5)                    │
     │ - MIS-eligibility + leverage table: per-symbol allowed leverage, so     │
     │ sizing never assumes 5× where the broker gives 1×. (A4)                 │
     │ - Extend cost_model usage to segment='equity_intraday' throughout —     │
     │ it's already correct, just needs wiring.                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ E4 — Pre-registration, walk-forward, verdict ← the go/no-go             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Order is not negotiable. This project already committed the CRITICAL-2  │
     │ error once (thresholds chosen after seeing Run 2/3 results) and fixed   │
     │ it in research/gate_criteria_preregistration.md. That discipline        │
     │ carries over.                                                           │
     │ 1. Write research/preregistration_intraday.md before any result exists: │
     │ universe rule, bar grain, horizons in bars, stop/target parameters,     │
     │ session-boundary and purge/embargo settings, fill and participation     │
     │ assumptions, cost via compute_charges, and that gate.py thresholds are  │
     │ used verbatim. Both research/*preregistration*.md and gate.py are       │
     │ protected paths (foreman/policy.py:31-41) — lands via PR, reviewed by   │
     │ you.                                                                    │
     │ 2. Reuse walkforward.make_folds with bars as the unit. One addition     │
     │ required: folds must not split within a session, or a boundary at 11:00 │
     │ leaks morning into afternoon.                                           │
     │ 3. Reuse evaluation/gate.py completely unchanged — same exact binomial  │
     │ hit-rate test, same 0.15 drop fraction, same min_folds_required=10.     │
     │ 4. Write research/verdict_intraday.md: net expectancy per trade after   │
     │ costs, daily P&L distribution at ₹75,000 exposure, hit rate, gate       │
     │ PASS/FAIL — and how measured daily expectancy compares to both          │
     │ ₹1,500–2,000 and your E0 baseline.                                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ No threshold may be adjusted after seeing a result. If the edge doesn't │
     │ clear costs, that's the finding, and E5/E6 don't get built.             │
     │                                                                         │
     │ E5 — Risk and capital controls (new — closes A1, A2, A6, A7)            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Must exist before any real money. New                                   │
     │ src/stocksense/execution/risk_controls.py:                              │
     │ - Daily loss limit + hard kill switch (A1): a rupee cap derived from    │
     │ E0/E4 measured drawdown — e.g. stop suggesting for the day after 2      │
     │ stopped trades or −X% of equity. Non-overridable by the model.          │
     │ - Equity-at-risk sizing (A6): limits expressed as % of ₹15,000 at risk  │
     │ per trade and in aggregate, not portfolio weight. Adapt                 │
     │ risk_layer.apply_risk_limits rather than replace it; keep               │
     │ correlation_cluster_weight — three bank stocks at 5× is one leveraged   │
     │ bet, not three positions.                                               │
     │ - Regime stand-down (A7): a pre-registered rule for hostile conditions  │
     │ (e.g. index gap beyond a threshold, volatility regime) that suppresses  │
     │ suggestions. Committed before it's tested, like every other threshold.  │
     │ - Intraday tax (A2): extend optimizer/tax.py with                       │
     │ speculative-business-income treatment at slab rate + 4% cess, so the    │
     │ scorecard reports take-home, not gross. Statutory rates as named        │
     │ constants, matching the existing pattern.                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ E6 — Daily suggestion loop, paper-first                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Nightly (~20–40 min, unattended): fetch → grade yesterday's frozen    │
     │ suggestions → build features → retrain → champion vs challenger (the    │
     │ fresh model must beat the incumbent on gate.py criteria or it's         │
     │ archived and the old one keeps running) → scorecard.                    │
     │ Nightly retrain ≠ nightly re-validation. The full walk-forward is a     │
     │ weekly/monthly job, not nightly — re-running it every night and         │
     │ watching for a good result is slow overfitting.                         │
     │ - Market hours — the split that matters:                                │
     │   - The model is frozen at 09:15 and does not change until midnight.    │
     │ Re-training intraday on today's data has seen the answer; it looks      │
     │ brilliant in testing and loses money live.                              │
     │   - Re-scoring is continuous. Every 5 minutes the frozen model runs     │
     │ over fresh bars: 09:30 opening list (2–4 names, or none — a "nothing    │
     │ worth trading" day is correct behaviour), then new setups as they form  │
     │ at 10:15, 11:40, 13:20.                                                 │
     │   - Stop/target monitoring is continuous — optionally recover v1's      │
     │ intraday_stops.py 7-second poll once a live feed exists.                │
     │   - 15:15 hard flag — MIS auto-squares around 15:20; close on your own  │
     │ terms first.                                                            │
     │   - Rule in one line: re-scoring during the day is fine, re-training    │
     │ during the day is forbidden.                                            │
     │ - Reuse harness/loops.py record_predictions / grade_matured_predictions │
     │ / build_reconcile_graph — already freezes a feature_snapshot_hash so    │
     │ no suggestion can be quietly recomputed with hindsight.                 │
     │ - Paper-first. Suggestions are graded against realized intraday paths   │
     │ for a defined shadow period before informing real capital.              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Carried alongside — Foreman honesty fixes                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Verified still broken this session. Item 4 directly affects E4's        │
     │ verification:                                                           │
     │ 1. executor.py:104 returns "merged" — no merge code exists anywhere.    │
     │ Implement it or rename to pushed.                                       │
     │ 2. insert_goal / insert_ledger_entry / insert_protected_violation       │
     │ (store.py:637/656/668) have zero call sites — goals, build_ledger,      │
     │ protected_violations are all 0 rows; record_goal_result UPDATEs a row   │
     │ that never existed. Wire the writers.                                   │
     │ 3. budget.invocations never incremented — budget table 0 rows, cap can  │
     │ never fire.                                                             │
     │ 4. verifier.py:89-97 hardcodes the research gate to passed=True,        │
     │ delegating to foreman/tools/research.py which does not exist. Make it   │
     │ fail closed — otherwise E4's gate verification is a rubber stamp.       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ The audit surface — how you check it's working                          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Cadence: Daily                                                          │
     │ What: Scorecard: suggested vs taken vs realized, net of costs and tax   │
     │ What it proves: The only number that matters                            │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Cadence: Daily                                                          │
     │ What: Kill-switch log: did the loss limit fire, was it respected        │
     │ What it proves: The control is live, not decorative                     │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Cadence: Weekly                                                         │
     │ What: Frozen-snapshot spot check: re-run the grader on a past           │
     │ suggestion,                                                             │
     │ confirm the hash matches                                                │
     │ What it proves: Nothing was recomputed with hindsight                   │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Cadence: Weekly                                                         │
     │ What: Champion/challenger log                                           │
     │ What it proves: Replacing nightly = unstable. Never replacing =         │
     │ learning                                                                │
     │ stalled                                                                 │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Cadence: Weekly                                                         │
     │ What: Feature-importance drift                                          │
     │ What it proves: A sudden lean on a long-ignored feature is usually a    │
     │ data bug,                                                               │
     │ not an insight — tree models let you actually look                      │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Cadence: Monthly                                                        │
     │ What: Full walk-forward re-validation                                   │
     │ What it proves: Live results still match backtested expectancy          │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Cadence: Continuous                                                     │
     │ What: Data-staleness + decay alarms (A8, A9)                            │
     │ What it proves: The feed is alive and the edge hasn't decayed           │
     │ ────────────────────────────────────────                                │
     │ Cadence: Every commit                                                   │
     │ What: leakage suite, determinism suite, adversarial pass, gate.py diff  │
     │ empty                                                                   │
     │ What it proves: The rigor scaffolding is intact                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ What gets parked and why                                                │
     │                                                                         │
     │ D3 (delivery-horizon re-run) and D4 (F&O OI) are parked, not cancelled. │
     │ D1/D2 stay and keep earning their keep — the daily bhavcopy spine       │
     │ supplies the intraday pipeline's point-in-time universe, its            │
     │ liquidity/price floors, its circuit bands, and its prior-day context    │
     │ features.                                                               │
     │                                                                         │
     │ One ambiguity worth naming: "delivery" means two things. You said stop  │
     │ looking at delivery trades (CNC/positional) — that's D3, parked. But    │
     │ delivery-% (bhavcopy_delivery, currently 0 rows) is a feature: the      │
     │ fraction of volume taken to delivery. Low delivery-% means a stock is   │
     │ being churned intraday — one of the more plausible genuinely-Indian     │
     │ intraday signals. Keep that backfill as an E2 feature. It is not        │
     │ positional trading; it measures how intraday a stock is behaving.       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Not doing: live order placement, autonomous trading, F&O trading,       │
     │ threshold re-derivation after seeing results.                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ Verification                                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - E0: kundli produces a report from your real tradebook; FIFO           │
     │ reconstruction reconciles to the broker's reported P&L within rounding. │
     │ Golden-file test for the Angel parser.                                  │
     │ - E1: a known session returns exactly 375 1-min bars, 09:15–15:29;      │
     │ fetched counts reconcile against cached files; unmapped symbols         │
     │ reported explicitly; interrupt-and-resume loses no DB progress (the     │
     │ 731c262 regression).                                                    │
     │ - E2: first-touch label verified against a hand-built path where stop   │
     │ precedes target and vice versa; no label window crosses a session       │
     │ boundary; volume-spike baseline verified time-of-day-aware.             │
     │ - E3: a fill is never priced at the signal bar's close; a deliberately  │
     │ oversized order is rejected by the participation cap; a circuit-locked  │
     │ bar is excluded; a 1×-leverage symbol is sized at 1×.                   │
     │ - E4: pre-registration commit timestamp precedes the first result; git  │
     │ diff on gate.py across the phase is empty; ≥10 folds; no fold boundary  │
     │ inside a session.                                                       │
     │ - E5: loss limit halts suggestions at the configured threshold in       │
     │ simulation; three correlated names collapse to one risk unit; tax       │
     │ figures reconcile against a hand-computed slab example.                 │
     │ - E6: a recorded suggestion's feature_snapshot_hash matches on          │
     │ re-grade; paper P&L reconciles against realized 1-min paths.            │
     │ - Full pytest tests/unit green (currently 398) plus remote CI via       │
     │ .github/workflows/verify.yml.                                           │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ Honest risks                                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. The intraday edge may not clear costs. At 8.3bps round-trip with     │
     │ daily turnover, cost drag is roughly an order of magnitude harsher      │
     │ relative to holding period than at the monthly horizon where alpha was  │
     │ already thin (+0.394%/month). Most likely failure mode, and E4 is       │
     │ designed to detect it rather than paper over it.                        │
     │ 2. 1-minute history starts in 2022. ~4.5 years, mostly one regime, no   │
     │ 2020-style crash. Fold counts will be healthy; regime diversity will    │
     │ not be.                                                                 │
     │ 3. Upstox bars ≠ Angel One fills. E3's fill model narrows this; it      │
     │ cannot close it. Real slippage is only knowable from real fills, which  │
     │ is what the paper period measures.                                      │
     │ 4. Leverage amplifies model error, not just returns. At 5×, three wrong │
     │ days ≈ 20% of the account. Sizing comes from measured drawdown          │
     │ (E0/E4), never from the profit target.                                  │
     │ 5. The target may be unreachable as a daily expectation. Covered in     │
     │ "The arithmetic". The system reports the real number.                   │
     │ 6. Tax turns a thin gross edge into a thinner net one. At slab rates,   │
     │ speculative income can take up to ~31% of profits. A strategy that      │
     │ clears costs by a small margin may not clear costs and tax — which is   │
     │ precisely why A2 is in scope rather than deferred.                      │
     ╰─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯

❯ /model sonnet                                                                 
  ⎿  Set model to claude-sonnet-5

      840 +    def write_intraday_bars(self, df: pd.DataFrame) -> int:   
      841 +        cols = ["symbol", "ts", "interval", "open", "high", "l
          +ow", "close", "volume"]                                       
      842 +        self.con.register("_ib", df[cols])                    
      843 +        self.con.execute(                                     
      844 +            f"""                                              
      845 +            INSERT INTO intraday_bars ({', '.join(cols)}) SELE
          +CT {', '.join(cols)} FROM _ib                                 
      846 +            ON CONFLICT (symbol, ts, interval) DO UPDATE SET  
      847 +                open = excluded.open, high = excluded.high, lo
          +w = excluded.low,                                             
      848 +                close = excluded.close, volume = excluded.volu
          +me                                                            
      849 +            """                                               
      850 +        )                                                     
      851 +        self.con.unregister("_ib")                            
      852 +        return len(df)                                        
      853 +                                                              
      854 +    def read_intraday_bars(                                   
      855 +        self, symbols: list[str] | None = None, start: str | N
          +one = None, end: str | None = None,                           
      856 +        interval: str = "1minute",                            
      857 +    ) -> pd.DataFrame:                                        
      858 +        query = "SELECT * FROM intraday_bars WHERE interval = 
          +?"                                                            
      859 +        params: list = [interval]                             
      860 +        if symbols:                                           
      861 +            query += f" AND symbol IN ({', '.join(['?'] * len(
          +symbols))})"                                                  
      862 +            params += symbols                                 
      863 +        if start and end:                                     
      864 +            query += " AND ts BETWEEN ? AND ?"                
      865 +            params += [start, end]                            
      866 +        return self.con.execute(query + " ORDER BY symbol, ts"
          +, params).fetchdf()

          +)}) SELECT {', '.join(cols)} FROM _uim

          +d.instrument_key, resolved = excluded.resolved                
      876 +            """

      878 +        self.con.unregister("_uim")

          +nt_map ORDER BY symbol").fetchdf()

      480 +    """Backfill Upstox 1-minute intraday bars into the store (
          +Phase E1),                                                    
      481 +    symbol by symbol, month-window by month-window, genuinely 
          +resumable

      486 +    re-run never re-downloads a window, and the DB already has

          +operty

          +l date --

     ~\.claude\plans\read-stocksense-1-txt-and-build-parallel-squirrel.md · 
     /plan to edit
     StockSense — Phase F: the desktop control center

     Date: 2026-08-18
     Supersedes: nothing — this is additive to Phase E. Phase E's status is
     summarized at the end for reference; E1/E2/Foreman-fixes are done and E3–E6
     continue as their own work, now with a UI to trigger and watch them
     instead of a terminal.

     ---
     Context

     You don't want to open Claude Code's terminal again. Everything StockSense
     can do — backfills, statement ingestion, Kundli, model training, the
     Foreman's agent-driven self-improvement — currently only exists as
     stocksense <command> typed into a shell, plus one read-only Electron
     dashboard that shows results after the fact but can't trigger anything.

     You want the Electron app to become the actual control surface: every
     capability triggerable from the UI, live status while it runs, an explicit
     authorize/decline gate for the one thing that touches your Claude account,
     model/effort/settings that live in the UI instead of source constants, and
     a genuine console feel — not a generic AI-app dashboard. The self-improving
     agent-harness logic (auto model switching, auto effort scaling, auto
     plan/auto mode) is explicitly separate, later work — this phase builds the
     manual controls that a future auto-policy would eventually drive, not the
     policy itself.

     What I found before planning this (verified, not assumed)

     The existing desktop app is a smaller, more solid foundation than STATUS.md
     suggested. One commit (4499315) built it: vanilla HTML/CSS/JS, zero
     dependencies beyond electron/electron-builder, an already-good dark
     "terminal aesthetic" (desktop/renderer/terminal.css), and a working
     two-process architecture — main.js spawns python -m stocksense.cli.main 
     serve as a child process, health-polls it, then loads the renderer pointed
     at that port. Every exit path kills the Python child cleanly (taskkill /pid
     <pid> /f /t on Windows, since plain .kill() is unreliable there).
     STATUS.md's "never rendered — ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 blocks it" was the
     coding agent's own sandbox environment, not a repo bug — that variable
     isn't set anywhere in the repo, only referenced in prose describing why the
     agent itself couldn't see a GUI window. On your actual machine this should
     just run. No "Brain tab" or "Live tab" exist in current code despite being
     mentioned in old notes — the only panel today is a single read-only
     dashboard (P&L, doshas, harness, registry, agent runs), polled every 2s.

     The FastAPI server is deliberately read-only today, by explicit design in 
     its own docstring — 8 GET endpoints, no POST, no WebSocket, no auth, no
     CORS, safety coming entirely from binding 127.0.0.1-only. This is the
     central thing this phase changes: it becomes a controlled read-write
     surface, with the new safety story being the authorize/decline gate (for
     anything Claude-related) plus explicit per-action confirmation in the UI,
     replacing "can't do anything" with "can do things, deliberately."

     No command currently has real trigger-and-watch infrastructure. Only
     reconcile runs through the harness's job_runs-backed graph runner; foreman 
     run, train-candidate, and every backfill are plain subprocesses or loops
     with no progress signal beyond their own stdout. There is also no foreman 
     stop despite budget.py's KillSwitch docstring referencing one — killing a
     running Foreman goal today means killing the OS process by hand.

     Claude Code CLI exposes no numeric usage/limit API. claude auth status 
     --json gives loggedIn/email/subscriptionType, nothing about tokens or
     session windows. What is real and verified: every session transcript under
     ~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl (608MB, 186 files, confirmed on this machine)
     carries a message.usage object with
     input_tokens/output_tokens/cache_*_tokens and a timestamp on every
     assistant turn — the same data community tools like ccusage parse. A local
     aggregator over these files gives real, measured rolling-window usage; it
     cannot give a certified "% of official limit" because Anthropic doesn't
     publish that formula for Pro/Max plans. Built as the "measured, clearly
     labeled" gauge per your decision below.

     Settings are already environment-variable-driven (pydantic-settings,
     STOCKSENSE_* prefix, freshly re-read on every get_settings() call, no
     in-memory caching to invalidate) — a live settings editor just needs to
     write .env and the next CLI/server invocation picks it up, no architecture
     change needed there. What's currently hardcoded and not settings-driven:
     per-role Claude model/effort (PLANNER_MODEL/CODEGEN_MODEL etc. are local
     constants in planner.py/codegen.py/assess.py, not Settings fields), and the
     Foreman's daily invocation cap (DEFAULT_MAX_INVOCATIONS_PER_DAY = 200 in
     budget.py, not overridable).

     Your statement files are Angel One exports, not yet parseable by the 
     existing parsers. Found in statements/: YourStatement_AACI179838.xlsx
     (Broking Ledger + Charges), Trades_History_AACI179838.xlsx
     (TradesAndCharges), ProfitLoss_Statement_AACI179838.xlsx (Equity P&L + F&O
     P&L), Trading_Insights.xlsx. Only Zerodha and Upstox parsers exist today
     (statements/parsers/); reading these directly shows Unnamed: 0-style
     columns, meaning each sheet has report-metadata preamble rows before the
     real header row — the existing _load_raw (parsers/base.py:47-48) assumes
     row 0 is the header and will misparse these as-is. .xlsx itself is already
     supported.

     ---
     Decisions taken

     Decision: Usage gauge
     Choice: Measured rolling 5h/7d token+message counts from local session
     logs,
     explicitly labeled as measured-not-official, with a user-configurable
     soft-alarm threshold (you calibrate it from experience, not from a number
     Anthropic never gives us)
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Decision: Frontend
     Choice: Extend the existing vanilla HTML/CSS/JS app — no framework, no
     build
     step, same terminal aesthetic, more panels + a small hand-rolled router and

     WebSocket client
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Decision: Server model
     Choice: FastAPI server becomes read-write (a deliberate, documented
     reversal of
     its current read-only docstring), owns subprocess/job management in Python
     where it's testable with the existing pytest discipline — Electron's
     main.js
     stays exactly as-is, still just spawning/killing the one server process
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Decision: Claude auth
     Choice: StockSense never touches ~/.claude/.credentials.json or any token.
     It
     only ever calls claude auth status --json (read-only) to detect who's
     logged
     in, gates its own Claude-invoking code paths behind a local
     authorize/decline
      flag, and auto-revokes that flag the moment the detected account changes
     ────────────────────────────────────────
     Decision: Kundli when declined
     Choice: Graceful degradation: numbers-only report (diagnostics, positions,
     counterfactuals — all pure Python) when Claude access isn't authorized; the

     narrative paragraph is the only part that's skipped

     ---
     Architecture

     Electron (unchanged: main.js spawns/kills ONE process)
       │
       ├─ renderer/  (vanilla JS panels, WebSocket client, fetch-based API
     client)
       │
       └─ spawns ──▶ FastAPI server (server/app.py — becomes read-write)
                       ├─ existing 8 GET endpoints (kept, unchanged)
                       ├─ NEW: POST /api/jobs/{command}      — spawn a CLI
     command as a tracked subprocess
                       ├─ NEW: POST /api/jobs/{job_id}/stop   — terminate a
     running job
                       ├─ NEW: GET  /api/jobs                 — list job history
     + live status
                       ├─ NEW: WS   /ws/jobs/{job_id}          — live stdout
     stream for one job
                       ├─ NEW: GET/PUT /api/settings            — read/write
     Settings fields (writes .env)
                       ├─ NEW: GET  /api/claude/auth             — proxies
     `claude auth status --json`
                       ├─ NEW: POST /api/claude/authorize        — set/clear the
     local access flag
                       ├─ NEW: GET  /api/claude/usage             —
     rolling-window usage from local logs
                       └─ every job spawns `python -m stocksense.cli.main
     <command> <args>`,
                          i.e. the SAME CLI surface you'd type by hand — no new
     business logic
                          duplicated between CLI and server, the server is
     purely an orchestrator

     The server never re-implements a command's logic — it always shells out to
     the existing, already-tested stocksense CLI (same pattern
     agent/claude_cli.py already uses for the claude binary: shutil.which,
     subprocess.run/Popen). This keeps one source of truth: the CLI stays fully
     usable by hand if you ever want it, and the UI is just another caller.

     ---
     F1 — Job management backend (the foundation everything else needs)

     New: src/stocksense/server/jobs.py
     - JobRegistry — spawns a CLI command via subprocess.Popen (not .run, so
     stdout can be streamed live), tracks PID, start time, status, captures
     stdout/stderr incrementally into a ring buffer + persists full output to
     disk on completion.
     - New table ui_jobs(job_id, command, args_json, pid, status, started_at, 
     finished_at, log_path) in store.py, following the existing job_runs pattern
     (store.py:76-83) but durable across server restarts — an in-memory-only
     registry would lose track of a still-running multi-hour backfill if the
     server itself restarts.
     - stop_job(job_id) — sends SIGTERM (POSIX) / taskkill /pid <pid> /t
     (Windows, matching main.js's existing kill pattern) to the tracked PID.
     This is also what finally implements the foreman stop capability
     budget.py's KillSwitch docstring already describes but never got wired to
     anything.
     - A command allowlist (explicit dict of {name: cli_subcommand_template}),
     never arbitrary string execution from the renderer — the UI can only ever
     trigger a specifically-registered command with typed parameters, matching
     the same "closed set, no hallucinated/injected commands" discipline
     foreman/tools/base.py's ToolRegistry already uses for agent tools.

     New: src/stocksense/server/app.py additions — POST /api/jobs/{command},
     POST /api/jobs/{job_id}/stop, GET /api/jobs, WS /ws/jobs/{job_id} (streams
     new stdout lines as they arrive; on connect, first replays the ring buffer
     so a UI opened mid-job isn't blank).

     Backfill progress, closing the gap the audit found:
     backfill-nse-archive/-corporate-actions/-intraday are plain loops with zero
     job_runs writes today — a UI watching only ui_jobs.status would see
     "running" for hours with no finer signal. Add a lightweight periodic
     progress line to each backfill loop (e.g. PROGRESS: 3412/6500 (52%) printed
     to stdout every N days processed) that the job console parses and renders
     as a progress bar — cheap, no schema change, and the raw log is still there
     underneath for anyone who wants it.

     ---
     F2 — Claude connection: authorize/decline, no stored credentials

     New: src/stocksense/agent/access.py
     - check_claude_auth() -> ClaudeAuthStatus — runs claude auth status --json,
     returns logged_in, email, plan. Read-only, never touches
     .credentials.json.
     - is_access_granted(store) -> bool / grant_access(store, email) /
     revoke_access(store) — a single boolean + the approved email, in a new
     app_settings key-value table (also backs F4's live settings). Detection, 
     not storage: on every check, if check_claude_auth().email != 
     stored_approved_email, access is automatically revoked and the UI must
     re-prompt — this is what makes "my Pro account may change" safe by
     construction rather than by remembering to handle it.
     - require_claude_access(store) — the single enforcement point. Raises a
     typed ClaudeAccessNotGranted if called while access isn't granted. Wired
     into every code path that currently calls agent.claude_cli.invoke:
     foreman/planner.py, foreman/codegen.py, foreman/assess.py,
     statements/report.py's narrative step, rag's ask command. One guard, can't
     be forgotten per-caller because each caller already funnels through invoke
     — actually enforced inside agent/claude_cli.invoke itself as the true
     single choke point, not duplicated at every caller.

     UI (panels/claude-connect.js): shows live auth status (email, plan) via GET
     /api/claude/auth; an explicit Authorize / Decline control (not a silent
     default) that calls POST /api/claude/authorize; every other panel's
     Claude-dependent actions (Foreman run, Kundli narrative, RAG ask) are
     visibly disabled with an explanatory tooltip when access isn't granted,
     rather than failing confusingly when clicked.

     ---
     F3 — Usage gauge (measured, labeled honestly)

     New: src/stocksense/agent/usage_tracker.py
     - Incremental, not a full 608MB re-parse every poll: tracks (file_path, 
     byte_offset) per session file in a small cache table, only reads
     newly-appended bytes on each check. Extracts message.usage + timestamp from
     type: "assistant" lines across all ~/.claude/projects/**/*.jsonl, not just
     this project — your Claude usage is one account-wide pool, not per-repo.
     - Rolling-window aggregation: trailing 5 hours and trailing 7 days, summed
     input/output/cache tokens, message count, session count, model mix.
     - New table claude_usage_events (or an appended parquet file, decided
     during implementation based on query pattern) — durable so a restart
     doesn't lose the rolling window's history.
     - GET /api/claude/usage returns the current windows plus your
     self-configured soft-alarm threshold (a Settings/app_settings field you set
     once you've learned where you actually get rate-limited) and whether it's
     currently tripped.

     UI: a persistent small gauge widget (always visible, not a separate screen
     you have to navigate to, similar to how the existing dashboard's
     clock/db-status header works) — bars for 5h/7d, token+message counts, model
     mix, and the soft-alarm state if set. Labeled (measured, not official) in
     the UI itself, not just in this plan.

     ---
     F4 — Live settings, not source constants

     New: app_settings table (key-value, shared with F2's access flag) + GET/PUT
     /api/settings.
     - Every core/config.py Settings field becomes editable: writes to .env (the
     mechanism pydantic-settings already reads fresh on every get_settings()
     call — verified no caching to invalidate, so this needs no server restart).
     - New Settings fields for what's currently hardcoded per-caller:
     planner_model/planner_effort, codegen_model/codegen_effort,
     assess_model/assess_effort, kundli_narrative_model/effort,
     foreman_max_invocations_per_day (replacing budget.py's hardcoded 200).
     planner.py/codegen.py/assess.py/budget.py read these instead of local
     constants.
     - UI (panels/settings.js): grouped forms — Data & Universe (liquidity
     floors, price source), Claude Roles (model/effort dropdowns per role,
     populated from claude --help's known effort levels so the UI can't offer an
     invalid one), Foreman (budget cap, max attempts), Risk (placeholders wired
     in as E5 lands). Every field shows its current value and a "reset to
     default" action; nothing silently falls back to a value you can't see.

     ---
     F5 — The panels: everything triggerable

     Each panel is a thin JS module (desktop/renderer/panels/*.js) calling F1's
     job API plus whatever read-only endpoints already exist. All reuse F1's
     job-console component for anything long-running.

     - pipeline.js — Data & Backfills: trigger
     backfill-nse-archive/-corporate-actions/-intraday/index-corpus with
     date-range inputs, live progress bar (from F1's parsed progress lines),
     stop button, run history.
     - statements.js — Statements & Kundli: file picker (defaults to scanning
     statements/) → statement-ingest → kundli, inline report view (numbers
     always, narrative section only if F2 access is granted).
     - research.js — Model & Research: train-candidate, predict, registry,
     tax-summary, and (as E3–E6 land) the intraday sweep, pre-registration
     viewer, and gate-result display slot in here as new buttons against the
     same job API — no new frontend plumbing needed when that backend work
     lands.
     - foreman.js — Agent Harness (manual control only, per your explicit
     split): goal text input + model/effort dropdowns (from F4's settings) →
     foreman run; foreman assess/status/ledger as read views; the new stop
     button from F1. This panel exposes the knobs; it does not decide anything
     on its own — no auto-switching logic lives here.
     - claude-connect.js — F2 + F3 combined: auth status, authorize/decline,
     usage gauge, per-role model/effort shortcut into F4.
     - settings.js — F4's forms.
     - dashboard.js — kept as-is (existing
     P&L/doshas/harness/registry/agent-runs panel), becomes the landing view.

     Job console — a persistent drawer/pane (not a separate screen — always one
     keystroke away, matching "terminal kinda ui but interactive"): live-streams
     whatever job is currently running via F1's WebSocket, shows recent job
     history, lets you re-open the log of a finished job. This is the closest
     thing to "still feels like a terminal" while every action that fills it is
     a button, not a typed command.

     Visual direction, stated explicitly since you called it out: extend
     terminal.css as-is — dark background, monospace-forward, information-dense
     grid panels, status color-coding (green/amber/red, functional not
     decorative), no gradients, no glassmorphism, no generic SaaS-dashboard
     chrome. If a panel doesn't clearly answer "what is this number/control
     for," it doesn't ship.

     ---
     Build order

     1. E0 first DONE. parsers/angel.py built (dynamic header-row/sheet
     detection, not a fixed skiprows — Angel's preamble length isn't stable),
     registered in PARSERS, 10 golden-file tests. Ingested the real tradebook
     (491 fills) and ran kundli for real. Found and fixed two real,
     previously-invisible bugs in agent/claude_cli.py along the way (both would
     have silently broken every multi-line Claude CLI call on Windows): (1) the
     prompt was passed as a -p <prompt> argv element, which cmd.exe's wrapping
     of the .cmd shim truncates at the first newline — fixed by sending it via
     stdin; (2) --setting-sources was given a raw path instead of one of its
     actual valid values (user/project/local) — fixed to "project". Both
     reproduced directly and covered by regression tests. Reconciled against
     Angel's own independent "Trading Insights" analytics for the overlapping
     window (within 1.6%, gap explained by 14 delivery trades mine includes that
     theirs excludes). Real numbers now in hand: 444 closed positions, net P&L
     −₹1,309.74 (gross +₹2,482.66, erased by 153% cost-drag from 40+
     trades/day), 63% concentration in one symbol, 45.3% win rate. These are
     what E5's risk/sizing calibrates to. 445/445 tests passing.
     2. F1 (job backend) — next. Nothing else in this phase can be demoed
     without it.
     3. F2 + F4 together (auth gate and settings share the app_settings table
     and touch the same code paths).
     4. F3 (usage gauge) — independent, can slot in anytime after F1.
     5. F5 (panels) — incrementally, dashboard/pipeline/statements first
     (fastest payoff, exercise the new job infra),
     foreman/claude-connect/settings next, research panel last since E3–E6's
     backend pieces are still landing.

     Phase E's intraday research (E3 execution realism, E4
     pre-registration+sweep, E5 risk controls, E6 daily loop) continues in
     parallel/after — once F1–F5 land, those become buttons in
     research.js/foreman.js instead of terminal commands, but the research work
     itself is unchanged by this phase.

     ---
     Verification

     - F1: a triggered backfill-corporate-actions --start ... --end ... (short
     range) shows live progress in the UI, a stop mid-run actually kills the OS
     process (confirmed via tasklist), ui_jobs survives a server restart with
     the job's last known status intact.
     - F2: revoking Claude Code's own login (claude auth logout) is detected on
     the next status check and the UI's authorized flag flips off automatically;
     a Kundli run with access declined produces a report with no narrative
     section and no crash.
     - F3: usage numbers for a known short Claude CLI call match a manual count
     from that session's own JSONL file; a restart doesn't reset the rolling
     window (durable table, not in-memory).
     - F4: changing a model dropdown in Settings and re-running foreman assess
     uses the new model — confirmed via agent_runs.input_json, not just "the UI
     says so."
     - F5: every panel's primary action round-trips: trigger → job appears in
     the console → completes → result visible in the relevant read panel (e.g. a
     train-candidate run updates research.js's registry view).
     - Full pytest tests/unit green throughout — every new Python module
     (jobs.py, access.py, usage_tracker.py) gets the same test discipline as the
     rest of the codebase (currently 432 passing). Electron/JS side gets manual
     verification per the project's "run and screenshot" convention, since
     there's no JS test runner in this stack today and adding one is out of
     scope here.

      345 +    if not path.exists():                                     
      346 +        return {}                                             
      347 +    values = {}

      349 +        line = line.strip()

          +:                                                             
      351 +            continue

      353 +        values[k.strip()] = v.strip()

          +t --

          +ry line

● Updated plan
