● Phase F control center — built and tested

  509/509 tests passing (up from 398 at session start). Everything from the
  approved plan is built:

  F1 — Job backend. New server/jobs.py: a closed command allowlist (never
  arbitrary execution), spawns CLI commands as tracked subprocesses, live stdout
  capture, single-job enforcement (DuckDB's real single-writer constraint, made
  explicit rather than a confusing lock error), a stop path, durable ui_jobs
  tracking. Backfill loops now print throttled PROGRESS: n/total (pct%) lines —
  confirmed working live in the actual running backfill.

  F2 — Claude authorize/decline. New agent/access.py: never touches your
  credentials file, only calls claude auth status. Enforced at the single choke
  point inside invoke() itself, so no caller can bypass it. Account-change 
  auto-revokes — the core safety property you asked for.

  F3 — Usage gauge. New agent/usage_tracker.py: incremental byte-offset scanning
  of your real session logs, rolling 5h/7d windows, labeled "measured, not
  official" in the UI itself.

  F4 — Live settings. Found and fixed a real bug here: my first pass wrote
  settings to a DB table Settings never reads — silently a no-op. Fixed to write
  .env (what get_settings() actually reads, verified directly). Per-role
  model/effort moved out of hardcoded constants in
  planner.py/codegen.py/assess.py/report.py/budget.py.

  F5 — The actual desktop UI. Six tabs (Dashboard, Pipeline, Statements,
  Foreman, Claude, Settings) extending the existing vanilla terminal-aesthetic
  app — no framework added. A persistent job console drawer streams live output
  over WebSocket. All 9 new JS files syntax-checked clean.

  What I verified for real, not just in mocks: started the actual FastAPI server
  and confirmed against real data — settings return your true values with
  secrets properly masked (••••••••8dc5), the statements/ folder lists your real
  4 files, and — importantly — every DB-touching endpoint correctly returned a
  clean 503 "database is busy" instead of crashing while the intraday backfill
  held the write lock. That degradation path works end-to-end, not just in
  tests.

  What I could not verify: the actual Electron window rendering. This sandbox
  has the same ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1 artifact documented earlier — I cannot see
  a GUI here. You'll need to run cd desktop && npm start yourself to confirm it
  looks right; I'd genuinely appreciate a screenshot or a quick "it works" so I
  know the visual side landed correctly.

  Backfill status: still running in the background, now at 18% through its retry
  pass (network dropped mid-run earlier, recovering the missed windows).

  Not done: Phase E's E3–E6 (execution realism, the pre-registered gate, risk
  controls, the daily loop) — those are next, and will now be triggerable from
  the Research/Foreman tabs once built rather than needing a terminal.

  Root cause: my intraday backfill is still running in the background (33%
  through its retry pass right now) and holds DuckDB's exclusive write lock the
  whole time it runs. Every /api/health call correctly returned 503 — that's the
  right behavior, not a bug in the API. The bug was in main.js: it refused to
  open the window until it got a 200, so it just retried in a tight loop for 15
  seconds and then crashed instead of showing you anything.

  Fixed: waitForHealth() now opens the window as soon as the API process
  responds at all — any status code, including 503 — since that already proves
  the server is alive and listening. The dashboard's own per-endpoint code
  (already in dashboard.js) shows "API unreachable" / "unavailable" gracefully

     │ Track E — Launchability and automation                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - E1. Desktop shortcut (you asked for this explicitly). Recommendation: │
     │ a launcher script + Windows shortcut, not an electron-builder           │
     │ installer. Reasoning, not preference: a packaged app would need a       │
     │ bundled Python interpreter and the src/ tree, while the data it         │
     │ operates on is a multi-GB local DuckDB file plus a data_store/ cache    │
     │ that cannot ship inside an installer. For a single-user tool whose      │
     │ backend and data live on this machine, a shortcut that runs the         │
     │ existing npm start from the repo gives you exactly the one-click access │
     │ you asked for with none of that fragility. Add [project.scripts] to     │
     │ pyproject.toml so stocksense works as a bare command too.               │
     │ - E2. Scheduler — only after E6 exists. reconcile is already            │
     │ date-idempotent and waiting for one. Windows Task Scheduler             │
     │ registration (via the existing job API) is the least-moving-parts       │
     │ option; an in-process scheduler would mean the app must be running at   │
     │ midnight, which defeats the purpose. Deliberately sequenced last: a     │
     │ nightly scheduler for a pipeline whose edge hasn't cleared the gate     │
     │ would be automating something not yet known to work.                    │

     │ Also worth doing                                                        │

     │ (you ran it), and the second should reflect Track E's actual            │
     │ sequencing.                                                             │
     │ - The 2s dashboard poll runs on every tab (dashboard.js:8,136) unlike   │
     │ the onTabActivate-gated panels. Gate it too.                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ ---                                                                     │
     │ Verification                                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - A1: trigger a short job, let it finish, reopen its log from history — │
     │ content matches data_store/ui_job_logs/{job_id}.log on disk.            │
     │ - A2: a backfill-corporate-actions run over a short range renders a     │
     │ moving bar, not raw PROGRESS: text.                                     │
     │ - A3: stop the API mid-session; panels show the real error, not         │
     │ "unavailable".                                                          │
     │ - A4: adding a throwaway entry to COMMANDS makes a form appear with no  │
     │ frontend edit.                                                          │
     │ - B/C: every newly-exposed command round-trips trigger → console →      │
     │ result visible in the relevant read panel; verdict_intraday.md is       │
     │ readable in the research tab without a terminal.                        │
     │ - D: if FAIL, the diagnosis distinguishes cost-drag from no-signal      │
     │ using alpha_gross vs alpha_net; the attempt counter increments and is   │
     │ visible before any second hypothesis is pre-registered.                 │
     │ - E1: double-clicking the shortcut opens the app on a machine where no  │
     │ terminal is already open.                                               │
     │ - Full pytest tests/unit green throughout (currently 552). Every new    │
     │ endpoint gets tests in the existing tests/unit/test_server_app_jobs.py  │
     │ pattern; /api/research/doc/{name} specifically gets a path-traversal    │
     │ test (it serves files by name).                                         │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Honest risks                                                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ 1. Track D's fail branch is the likely one. The 8-symbol smoke test     │
     │ showed negative net alpha on all 3 folds. That is not a reliable        │
     │ predictor of the full 244-symbol result either way, but it is not       │
     │ encouraging, and the plan is deliberately written so that a FAIL        │
     │ produces useful work rather than a scramble.                            │
     │ 2. The attempt counter is a constraint on ourselves, and will feel like │
     │ friction exactly when it matters most — right after a disappointing     │
     │ result, when trying "just one more configuration" is most tempting.     │
     │ That is precisely when it earns its keep.                               │
     │ 3. Track E2 automates a pipeline whose edge is unproven. Sequenced last │
     │ for that reason; building it earlier would be automating a hope.        │

      200 +    """Phase G/B3: `index-corpus` was already triggerable from
          + the                                                          
      201 +    Pipeline tab, but nothing could query the corpus it builds
          + -- no

      203 +    agent.claude_cli.invoke(), so the F2 authorize/decline gat
          +e applies

          +th; this

          +actly one

          +"

      208 +

      210 +    if not question:                                          
      211 +        raise HTTPException(status_code=422, detail="'question
          +' is required")                                               
      212 +                                                              
      213 +    store = _store()                                          
      214 +    try:                                                      
      215 +        return rag_ask(question, store)                       
      216 +    finally:                                                  
      217 +        store.close()

          + pre-                                                         
      265 +    registrations, verdicts, fold-result CSVs -- so E4's answe
          +r (and                                                        
      266 +    every future research doc) is readable from the UI without
          + a

          +e

          +h is the

          +pplied                                                        
      270 +    name."""                                                  
      271 +    folder = REPO_ROOT / "research"                           
      272 +    if not folder.exists():                                   
      273 +        return {"docs": []}                                   
      274 +    docs = sorted(                                            
      275 +        p.name for p in folder.iterdir()

      281 +@app.get("/api/research/doc/{name}")                          
      282 +def get_research_doc(name: str):                              
      283 +    """Serves one file's content by NAME, not by arbitrary pat
          +h --                                                          
      284 +    deliberately restricted to a single path segment (no '/' a
          +llowed)                                                       
      285 +    resolved against `research/` and then verified to still re
          +solve                                                         
      286 +    INSIDE that directory before ever being opened. Both check
          +s matter:                                                     
      287 +    rejecting '/' stops the obvious 'name=../../.env' case, bu
          +t                                                             
      288 +    resolving and re-checking containment is what actually sto
          +ps a                                                          
      289 +    symlink or a cleverer traversal from escaping the director
          +y --                                                          
      290 +    string-matching the input alone is not a sufic ient guard.
          +"""                                                           
      291 +    if "/" in name or "\\" in name or name in (".", ".."):    
      292 +        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="invalid d
          +ocument name")                                                
      293 +                                                              
      294 +    folder = (REPO_ROOT / "research").resolve()               
      295 +    candidate = (folder / name).resolve()                     
      296 +    if not candidate.is_relative_to(folder):                  
      297 +        raise HTTPException(status_code=400, detail="invalid d
          +ocument name")                                                
      298 +    if candidate.suffix.lower() not in RESEARCH_DOC_EXTENSIONS
          + or not candidate.is_file():                                  
      299 +        raise HTTPException(status_code=404, detail=f"no resea
          +rch document named {name!r}")                                 
      300 +                                                              
      301 +    return {"name": name, "content": candidate.read_text(encod
          +ing="utf-8", errors="replace")}                               
      302 +

      125 +# closes -- the E4 sweep's first fold ran 6.5+ hours before th
          +is fix). ----                                                 
      126 +                                                              
      127 +def test_precomputed_sessions_gives_identical_result_to_the_de
          +fault_path() -> None:                                         
      128 +    bars = pd.DataFrame([                                     
      129 +        _bar("X", "2026-01-05 09:15", 100, 100.5, 99.5, 100), 
      130 +        _bar("X", "2026-01-05 09:16", 100, 103, 99.8, 102),   
      131 +        _bar("X", "2026-01-05 09:17", 102, 104, 101, 103),    
      132 +    ])                                                        
      133 +    entries = pd.DataFrame([{"symbol": "X", "entry_ts": "2026-
          +01-05 09:15", "entry_price": 100.0}])                         
      134 +                                                              
      135 +    default_result = first_touch_label(bars, entries, stop_pct
          +=0.015, target_pct=0.02, max_holding_minutes=60)              
      136 +    sessions = precompute_sessions(bars)                      
      137 +    precomputed_result = first_touch_label(                   
      138 +        bars, entries, stop_pct=0.015, target_pct=0.02, max_ho
          +lding_minutes=60, sessions=sessions,                          
      139 +    )                                                         
      140 +    pd.testing.assert_frame_equal(default_result, precomputed_
          +result)                                                       
      141 +                                                              
      142 +                                                              
      143 +def test_precomputed_sessions_reused_across_many_calls_stays_c
          +orrect() -> None:                                             
      144 +    """The actual access pattern this fix targets: MANY separa
          +te                                                            
      145 +    first_touch_label calls (one per trade, as                
      146 +    simulate_intraday_trades_for_fold does) against the SAME  
      147 +    precomputed sessions dict -- each call must still get the 
          +right                                                         
      148 +    answer for its own entry, not bleed state from a previous 
          +call."""                                                      
      149 +    bars = pd.DataFrame([                                     
      150 +        _bar("A", "2026-01-05 09:15", 100, 100.2, 99.8, 100), 
      151 +        _bar("A", "2026-01-05 09:16", 100, 103, 99.8, 102),  #
          + A: target hit                                                
      152 +        _bar("B", "2026-01-05 09:15", 50, 50.1, 49.9, 50),    
      153 +        _bar("B", "2026-01-05 09:16", 50, 50.2, 48.0, 48.5),  
          +# B: stop hit                                                 
      154 +    ])                                                        
      155 +    sessions = precompute_sessions(bars)                      
      156 +                                                              
      157 +    entry_a = pd.DataFrame([{"symbol": "A", "entry_ts": "2026-
          +01-05 09:15", "entry_price": 100.0}])                         
      158 +    entry_b = pd.DataFrame([{"symbol": "B", "entry_ts": "2026-
          +01-05 09:15", "entry_price": 50.0}])                          
      159 +                                                              
      160 +    result_a = first_touch_label(bars, entry_a, stop_pct=0.02,
          + target_pct=0.015, sessions=sessions)                         
      161 +    result_b = first_touch_label(bars, entry_b, stop_pct=0.02,
          + target_pct=0.015, sessions=sessions)                         
      162 +                                                              
      163 +    assert result_a.iloc[0]["outcome"] == "target"            
      164 +    assert result_b.iloc[0]["outcome"] == "stop"

● Now let's check the gross vs net breakdown to diagnose why — cost drag vs no

● The full sweep is done. Here's the complete, honest picture.

  Verdict: GATE FAIL — decisively, not marginally

  0 of 15 folds were positive. Not "mostly negative" — every single fold, across
  the full 2022–2026 history and all 244 liquid symbols, came back negative.
  The hit-rate significance test literally cannot reject "this is random"
  (p=1.0, because there are zero positive folds to even test).

  Why — diagnosed without touching any threshold

  ┌─────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────────┐
  │                                 │            value            │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ Mean gross alpha (before costs) │ −0.018% — essentially zero  │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ Mean net alpha (after costs)    │ −0.1165%                    │
  ├─────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────────┤
  │ Cost drag per trade             │ 0.098% (~9.8bps round-trip) │
  └─────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────────┘

  This is the important part: the signal itself carries no real edge. Gross
  alpha is statistically indistinguishable from zero — before a single rupee of
  cost, the model's top-10 picks perform about the same as the average of the
  tradeable universe. Costs then turn that flat signal into a reliable loss.
  This is not primarily a "real edge, killed by execution costs" story (which
  would show positive gross alpha and only-negative net alpha) — it's a "the
  features and ranker aren't finding genuine cross-sectional edge at 5-minute
  intraday granularity" story. Hit rates sit at 34–39% across every fold too,
  well under half.

  What this means concretely

  The system correctly did its job: it built a rigorous, pre-registered,
  honestly-executed test of whether intraday signals on this feature set clear
  real trading costs — and the answer is no, cleanly. That's a real result, not
  a failure of the pipeline.

  Where things stand

  Per the plan, the next step (Track D, item 3) is building an
  evaluation-attempt counter before testing any second hypothesis — specifically
  so that if we try again with different features or parameters, we're not
  quietly p-hacking against the same holdout. That's cheap to build and useful
  either way.

  But the actual decision — try a new pre-registered hypothesis, or accept this
  and let StockSense finish as a research + statement-forensics tool without
  live intraday trading — is yours, not mine to make. What would you like to do?

● I need to stop and be very direct here before writing anything into a plan,

  The core problem

  The gate we just ran failed decisively — 0 of 15 folds positive, gross alpha
  essentially zero. That means: as of right now, there is no validated model or
  signal in this pipeline that has ever been shown to pick winning intraday
  stocks. None. The whole architecture of this project — pre-registration,
  walk-forward, the gate, "no LLM number decides a trade" — exists specifically
  so that a request like "give me 10 profitable stocks for tomorrow" either
  comes from a model that's earned that trust, or doesn't happen at all.

  If I used Claude to look at gainers/losers and "predict" 10 stocks by
  reasoning about them directly, that would not be the StockSense pipeline. It
  would be an LLM guessing, dressed up in the pipeline's UI and vocabulary,
  going into a real 5× leveraged trade with real money tomorrow morning. That's
  a materially different and much riskier thing than what you've described, and
  it's the exact failure mode this whole project's engineering discipline was
  built to prevent. I'm not going to generate that list, even if the pipeline
  could technically produce output — because right now, honestly, its output
  would carry no more information than a coin flip. Actually worse than a coin
  flip: the measured net expectancy was consistently negative.

  What I can actually and honestly do before tomorrow

  1. Backfill the missing recent days — real, safe, useful regardless of what
  you decide next. The intraday spine stops a few days short of today; catching
  it up is just data hygiene.
  2. Not produce speculative picks, gross-alpha-zero model or not.

  What building something real would actually take

  A genuinely validated intraday model isn't a same-night task — it would mean
  forming a new hypothesis (different features, different holding logic, maybe a
  different horizon), pre-registering it in writing before seeing any result
  (same discipline as before), running the full walk-forward again, and letting
  the gate judge it. That's realistically days, not hours, and it might fail
  again — that's the honest range of outcomes.

  What I'd suggest instead

  Don't trade intraday off this pipeline tomorrow morning. If you want to trade
  tomorrow on your own judgment, that's entirely your call and separate from
  what StockSense can currently back with evidence.
